Chapter 16

Chapter Notes

longest chapter to date, buckle up

Rio

Rio hadn’t had a moment to herself all weekend.

She had spent most of Friday night hunched over the toilet, retching until her throat burned, or curled up in bed, shivering beneath layers of blankets that did nothing to warm her. By Saturday morning, the nausea had dulled to an ache, but the exhaustion hung heavy, clinging to her like a second skin. Sleep had been impossible; every time she closed her eyes, the guilt, the cravings, or her own traitorous thoughts would claw their way to the surface.

Alice and Jen hadn’t left her alone. They hovered like first-time parents, fussing over every little thing – did she eat enough? Did she drink water? Did she need anything? They meant well; Rio knew that. But by Sunday, their constant hovering had started to grate on her nerves.

She’d snapped at Alice for checking her temperature, barked at Jen for suggesting that she should rest, and then spent the next hour feeling like the world’s biggest asshole.

Still, she couldn’t shake the feeling of being smothered. They were treating her like a child, like she didn’t already know she’d fucked up. She knew. She didn’t need the reminders, the worried looks, or the endless, suffocating concern.

The cravings hadn’t been as bad as she’d expected. There was an itch at the back of her neck, a dull, persistent thought whispering that one more time wouldn’t hurt. But she’d ignored it. It was easier with Alice and Jen hovering, even if their presence made her feel like she couldn’t breathe.

They’d both told her not to go to her session with Agatha today. Jen had been especially adamant, pacing back and forth in the living room, her arms crossed tight over her chest as she ranted about Agatha’s unprofessionalism. Alice had been quieter but no less angry, her sharp glances saying everything she didn’t voice.

But Rio couldn’t skip. Missing a session wasn’t just about Agatha – it was about her probation officer, about keeping her record clean and staying out of trouble. It was about Lilia, too. If Lilia found out she’d missed an appointment, the older woman would worry, and Rio couldn’t stomach the thought of disappointing her.

Still, a part of her wasn’t even sure if she was supposed to go. Agatha had told her to leave on Thursday, had made it clear she didn’t want to see Rio again. But then she’d shown up after Rio’s relapse, sitting on her bed like she cared. And then she’d left.

Rio decided to go anyway. If Agatha didn’t want her there, then fine – she could say so to Rio’s face. But Rio wasn’t about to give her the satisfaction of thinking she’d completely unraveled her. Agatha had seen her vulnerable, raw and exposed, and Rio hated it. She needed Agatha to know that it hadn’t broken her.

Alice and Jen had argued with her about going alone, of course. They didn’t trust Agatha, or Rio for that matter. But the last thing she wanted was them marching her to Agatha’s office like she couldn’t handle it herself. After promising she’d text once she arrived, they reluctantly let her go.

As she walked, the tension in her chest grew tighter, her steps heavier. She didn’t know what to expect from this session, didn’t know what she would say or how she would act. But one thing was clear: she couldn’t let Agatha see how much she’d hurt her.

The walk felt longer than usual. By now, she was familiar with the streets, what corners to turn and how to most efficiently get to Agatha’s office. But today, she wandered almost mindlessly, the entire walk became like a haze.

The stairs creaked under Rio’s boots as she made her way up to Agatha’s office. Her hands were jammed into her coat pockets, clenched into tight fists to keep from shaking. She hated it – the pit in her stomach, the itch at the back of her neck that wouldn’t quit, the way her brain wouldn’t shut up.

She stopped at the door, taking a breath to steady herself. It didn’t help. The moment she knocked, she felt her heart rate pick up. No answer. She knocked again, then pushed the door open.

The office looked the same as it always did – tidy, professional, with that faint smell of coffee and lavender that somehow made the space feel more personal than clinical. A light was on in the small kitchen, and she could hear the tap running.

Agatha was making coffee. That was… good. Normal. Rio could deal with normal.

She didn’t take off her coat. It felt like armor, a layer between her and everything she didn’t want to feel. Instead, she sank into the couch, her arms crossed tight, one hand creeping to scratch at the back of her neck. The craving was there, buzzing faintly, but it wasn’t just that. The pit in her stomach felt heavier.

Her teeth sank into her thumbnail as her thoughts raced. She hadn’t wanted to come here, hadn’t wanted to face Agatha again, but now that she was here, now that she was waiting for her to step out of the kitchen, it felt worse. The tap turned off, and Rio braced herself, stiffening.

But when the figure stepped out, it wasn’t Agatha.

It was a man.

Rio let out a startled yelp, her hand shooting out instinctively to grip the edge of the couch. Her body went tense, her mind scrambling to make sense of what the fuck was going on.

“Sorry,” the man said, his voice calm and smooth as he held up a hand in apology. There was a faint smile on his face, a glint of humor in his eyes that made Rio’s skin crawl. “Didn’t mean to startle you. I’m Dr. Stephen Strange, one of Agatha’s colleagues.”

Rio just stared at him, her heart hammering. “What the fuck?” she snapped before she could stop herself.

Dr. Strange’s smile widened slightly, and he chuckled – a sound that made Rio’s jaw clench. “I take it she didn’t tell you what’s going on.”

No shit.

Her silence must have been answer enough, because he tilted his head and offered her an almost sympathetic look that only pissed her off more. “Agatha requested your case be transferred. I’ll be handling your sessions moving forward.”

The words didn’t register at first. When they did, the fury came like a slap to the face. “What?”

“I understand this might come as a surprise-“

“You think?” Rio snapped, her voice rising. Her fingers twitched, curling into fists again. “She couldn’t even fucking tell me herself?”

Of all the things that Agatha could have done, this was not what she had expected. It was her that was supposed to be the one who didn’t want to come to sessions, not Agatha. How fucking dare she just quit like it all ment nothing?

Dr. Strange’s expression shifted, but not in the way Rio wanted. He didn’t look apologetic or guilty. If anything, he looked amused, and that only made her anger boil hotter.

“Look, I know this isn’t ideal,” he said, his tone infuriatingly calm. “But these things happen. Why don’t we take some time to get to know each other? Start fresh.”

“Yeah, no thanks,” Rio shot back, crossing her arms tighter over her chest. “You think I’m just gonna sit here and spill my guts to some random guy because she couldn’t handle it? Think again.”

He chuckled again, a sound that grated on her nerves like nails on a chalkboard. “I think we’ve gotten off on the wrong foot. Look, Rio, I get it. This is a big change. But I promise, I’m here to help.”

“Sure you are,” she muttered, her tone dripping with sarcasm. “Because that’s worked out so well for me in the past.”

His expression softened – like he thought he understood her, like he thought he could read her – and that just made her angrier.

“Well,” he said, “maybe you can tell me why you feel that way. We’ve got time.”

Rio leaned forward, her voice low and cold. “You wanna know why? Because Agatha dumped me on your doorstep like I’m someone else’s problem. That’s why.”

His calm demeanor didn’t crack, and that only pissed her off more. “She didn’t dump you,” he said patiently. “She made a professional decision to ensure you get the best care possible. And that’s where I come in.”

Rio barked a bitter laugh, leaning back again. “Yeah, good luck with that.”

Agatha hadn’t even had the guts to face her. Just passed her off to this guy and walked away. Fucking coward.

Her fingers itched again, her anger bubbling just below the surface. If Agatha thought this would solve anything, she was dead wrong.

Dr. Strange leaned back in his chair, adjusting his tie like he had all the time in the world. “Alright, Rio. Why don’t we start simple? Tell me about yourself.”

Rio would rather jump out the nearest window.

She scowled at him, deadpan. “Pass.”

He chuckled, the sound so light and unbothered that it made her blood boil. “Fair enough. How about I start? I’m Dr. Strange-“

“No shit,” Rio interrupted. “Got that part already.”

His lips twitched, clearly fighting a grin. “Right. Well, I’ve been working in this field for over ten years. I specialize in trauma and addiction, and I like to think I’m pretty good at what I do.”

“Congratulations,” she muttered, her tone flat. Dod he want a fucking round of applause?

“And,” he continued, undeterred, “I enjoy coffee, long hikes, and science documentaries. Your turn.”

As if the situation couldn’t get any worse, not only was the man sitting in front of her some kind of know-it-all, he was also a giant nerd. The universe had to be laughing at her.

She snorted. “What, am I supposed to say, ‘Hi, I’m Rio, and I don’t want to be here?’”

“If that’s how you’d like to introduce yourself,” he said smoothly, “I won’t stop you.”

Her fingers dug into her arms, nails pressing just hard enough to sting. She wasn’t about to give him the satisfaction of getting under her skin. “Fine. I’m Rio. I don’t like coffee, I don’t hike, and the only thing worse than science documentaries is sitting in this room with you.”

Dr. Strange laughed. Actually laughed. Like she’d just said something genuinely funny.

“Good to know,” he said, his tone maddeningly cheerful. “We’re making progress already.”

She rolled her eyes so hard it was a wonder they didn’t get stuck. “You’re fucking unbearable, you know that?”

“I’ve been told,” he said with a wink.

Her jaw clenched. “What, by all your other clients you annoyed into recovery?”

His smile widened. “Something like that.”

Her gaze flicked to his hands, noticing the faint tremor in the one holding the pen. “You always this shaky?”

Dr. Strange didn’t miss a beat. “Good eye. It’s from a car accident I was in years ago.”

Rio raised an eyebrow, her lip curling. “And they still let you drive? Guess they really will let anyone behind the wheel these days.”

His mouth quirked at the edges, like he was trying not to smile. “I actually don’t drive much anymore. But thanks for the concern.”

“Concern?” she scoffed, leaning forward now, her voice dripping with mockery. “Please. I was just wondering how many people you’ve mowed down with your shaky-ass hands.”

“Luckily, I don’t need my hands to be steady to help people,” he said lightly, the corner of his mouth twitching upward again.

“Oh, help people, huh?” she shot back. “You’re a regular fucking hero, aren’t you?” She tilted her head, narrowing her eyes at him. “You get off on it? Have some kind of white saviour kink?

“Fair enough,” he said, his lips twitching into that infuriating almost-smile again.

Her fingers curled into fists, her nails biting into her palms. She wanted to wipe that smug look off his face so badly it physically hurt. “You think this is funny?” she hissed.

“I think,” he said, meeting her glare with that same unbearable calm, “that you’re trying very hard to get a reaction out of me. And I think it’s because you’re upset, and it’s easier to lash out than to talk about what’s really bothering you.”

“Oh, fuck off with your psychoanalysis,” she snapped, throwing her hands in the air.

_____

By the time the session ended, Rio was quite proud of the fact she hadn’t throttled him. She should honestly be giving a medal for that. She slammed the door shut behind her and stormed down the street, fists clenched and jaw tight. The man’s infuriating patience, his stupid warm smile – it all made her want to scream. She imagined slashing his tires or spray- painting something obscene on his front door. Maybe ‘fuck you asshole’ in big red letters. That’d send a message.

She was so lost in her fantasies of petty revenge that she almost didn’t hear the car horn.

“Rio!”

She blinked and looked up, startled. A familiar car was parked at the curb, and Lilia was leaning out the driver’s side window.

What the hell?

“What are you doing here?” Rio asked, suspicion creeping into her voice.

“Get in the car,” Lilia said, her tone leaving no room for argument.

For a moment, Rio hesitated, debating whether to bolt or comply. She knew Lilia would only follow her though, and a car chase really wasn’t high up on her list of things to do today. Insted, she slid into the seat, slamming the door shut behind her.

“Okay, fine. What’s this about?” Rio asked, folding her arms. “You here to lecture me?”

Lilia glanced at her briefly, then back at the road as she pulled into traffic. “Are you hungry?”

The question threw her off. “What?”

“Are you hungry?” Lilia repeated, her voice calm, almost casual.

Rio blinked, utterly confused. She’d been bracing herself for a scolding, maybe even a full- blown intervention, not… this. “Uh, yeah, I guess.”

“Good,” Lilia said simply. “We’re going to get takeout. There’s a little Spanish place on Main Street that opened while you were doing time. You’ll like it.”

Rio stared at her, dumbfounded. “Wait, what? You’re not… mad at me?”

Lilia didn’t answer right away, her focus on the road. Finally, she said, “Why would I be mad at you?”

“I don’t know,” Rio said, her tone dripping with sarcasm. “Maybe because I’m a total screw- up? Because I relapsed and made a mess of everything?”

Lilia glanced at her again, her expression unreadable. “You’re not a screw-up Rio. And no, I’m not mad at you. I’m concerned because I can see that you’re struggling, and I hate to see you hurting.”

Rio didn’t know how to respond to that.

Lilia kept her eyes on the road, the hum of the engine filling the silence between them. She glanced at Rio for a moment, studying the way her foot was tapping on the dashboard. “So, how was therapy?”

Rio didn’t immediately respond, instead, kicking the dash again absentmindedly with her foot. Lilia shot her a quick glance. “Watch it with the foot, kid,” she said fondly, her voice light.

Rio huffed, leaning back in her seat. “It was a fucking disaster, Lilia. Agatha fucking passed me off to this guy, Dr. Strange, or whatever the hell his name is. He’s the most annoying person I’ve ever met in my life.”

Lilia didn’t seem surprised by the rant. She nodded, as if she’d been expecting it. “That must’ve been frustrating,” she said, her voice unwavering.

Rio snorted, her anger simmering as she crossed her arms. “He’s all ‘let’s build a bond, let’s get to know each other.’ Like, fuck off. I’m not some project you can fix.”

Lilia hummed, contemplating. “You should let me do a Tarot reading for you soon, it’s been a while.”

Rio rolled her eyes. “I’m convinced it’s rigged. Every time I try, I always get the death card. Like, what kind of answer is that?”

Lilia chuckled, but her voice was reassuring. “Well, maybe the cards are just trying to tell you something. Death doesn’t always mean the end, you know. Could be about transformation. Change.”

The rest of the drive was relatively quiet. Rio closed her eyes, leaning back against the headrest and listening to the slight hum of the car along with Lilia’s mild road rage. She even found herself chuckling, despite everything, when Lilia muttered under her breath at someone who drove too slow.

Eventually, they arrived at the restaurant. Lilia stepped out of the car, leaving Rio alone with her thoughts as she headed into the Spanish restaurant to pick up their takeout. Rio leaned her head back against the seat, exhaling sharply. She fiddled with the sleeve of her sweater.

Pulling her phone out of her pocket, she opened her messages and started typing furiously to Alice and Jen.

Rio: You seriously told Lilia?! What hell is wrong with you two?

Rio: Next time, how about NOT ratting me out?

Satisfied – but still irritated – Rio tossed her phone onto the passenger seat and folded her arms. But the silence persisted, gnawing at her. With a groan, she picked the phone back up. “What else am I supposed to do?” she muttered to herself, opening the browser. She typed ‘Dr. Stephen Strange psychiatrist’ into the search bar.

The results made her immediately regret the decision.

Pages and pages of accolades, interviews, and glowing reviews about the man filled her screen. Articles hailed him as a “pioneer in psychiatric methods” and “one of the most highly sought-after professionals in the field.” One headline read, Dr. Strange: The Miracle Mind Healer.

Rio grimaced, her lips curling in disdain. “Miracle healer, my ass,” she muttered. His only achievement was being a cis white man in a patriarchal society. She kept scrolling, her irritation only growing as she saw his smirking face staring back at her in every photo. He looked every bit the polished, self-important professional she imagined – and she hated him even more for it.

“Of course he’s a fucking genius,” she muttered bitterly, closing her phone and tossing it aside.

Lilia returned a moment later, balancing a couple of large bags of food in her hands. “I come bearing gifts,” she announced cheerfully, sliding into the car and setting the bags on Rio’s lap. “Still warm and smells amazing.”

Rio grunted in acknowledgment, the aroma of saffron and spices wafting up. Her stomach growled despite herself.

The drive back to Rio’s apartment was uneventful, and they carried the food inside together.

The apartment, as usual, was in a state of disarray. Dishes were piled up in the sink, clothes were draped over the couch, and empty takeout containers littered the coffee table.

“Rio,” Lilia sighed, putting the bags down and gesturing around. “This place looks like a hurricane hit it.”

“It’s fine,” she muttered, avoiding her gaze. “I’ll clean it up later.”

Lilia raised an eyebrow but didn’t press. They spread out the food containers, the scents filling the room and making Rio’s mouth water. They sat on the couch and dug in. The flavors were rich - familiar and comforting.

“This is good,” she admitted, though her tone was begrudging.

“Of course it is,” Lilia said with a smirk, tearing off a piece of bread. “I only bring you the best.”

They ate mostly in silence, the only sounds the occasional clatter of cutlery and the rustle of takeout wrappers. As they finished up, Rio leaned back against the couch, her stomach full. Maybe if she kept quiet Lilia would forget why she was here.

Lilia wiped her hands with a napkin, her gaze shifting to Rio. “Alright,” she said gently. “Let’s talk.”

There went that plan. She fought the urge to groan.

Rio tensed, her fingers twitching at her sides. “Talk about what?” she said flatly, though they both knew the answer.

Lilia gave her a warning glance. “What do you want me to say?” Rio muttered. “That I fucked up? You already know that.”

Lilia sighed softly, watching Rio’s defensive posture tighten further. “Rio,” she said gently, “I can see you’re hurting. Let me help.”

“I don’t need help,” Rio snapped, her voice laced with anger. “I don’t need you, or Agatha, or this stupid Dr Strange telling me what’s wrong with me or how to fix it. I’m fine.”

“You’re not fine,” Lilia said calmly, unfazed by the bite in Rio’s tone. “And that’s okay.”

Rio scoffed, leaning back against the couch with her arms crossed. “God, why does everyone think they know me better than I know myself? I don’t need some - some intervention.”

“This isn’t an intervention,” Lilia said patiently. “This is me caring about you.”

“Well, don’t,” Rio said sharply, scowling at the table. “It’s not worth it. I’m not worth it. So how about you just fuck off.”

Lilia studied Rio for a long moment, her calm expression not shifting in the slightest. Then, without a word, she set her food down on the coffee table and turned to Rio, opening her arms wide.

“Come here,” she said simply, her tone leaving no room for argument.

Rio blinked. “I’m literally twenty-nine years old,” she said flatly, folding her arms over her chest. “What do you think you’re doing?”

“Shut your mouth and come here,” Lilia replied, her voice as warm as it was firm.

Rio stared at her for a beat, then rolled her eyes dramatically. “This is so stupid,” she muttered, but she shifted on the couch, moving closer.

“Keep talking, and I’ll make you sit on my lap,” Lilia said, and the threat was enough to shut Rio up. Begrudgingly, she let herself lean into Lilia’s embrace, her head resting awkwardly against Lilia’s shoulder.

Lilia’s arms wrapped around her, steady and sure. She began to rub Rio’s back in slow, soothing circles. “There,” she murmured softly. “That’s better, isn’t it?”

Rio didn’t respond immediately. She sat stiffly for a moment, but the warmth of Lilia’s hold and the repetitive motion on her back began to chip away at her defenses. Slowly, her shoulders dropped, and she let out a breath she hadn’t realized she was holding.

“I hate you for this,” Rio muttered half-heartedly.

“No, you don’t,” Lilia said with a small chuckle. “Now talk to me. What’s going on?”

Rio hesitated, the words caught in her throat. But something about being held, about not having to meet Lilia’s eyes which made it easier to speak.

“I told Agatha about Nicky.”

Lilia’s hand paused for a second before continuing its soothing motion. “Yeah?” she prompted gently.

“She flipped,” Rio said, her voice tight. “Told me she didn’t want to see me again. Said… she said she wished I’d died instead.”

“Oh, Rio,” Lilia whispered, her arms tightening around her.

“I knew she would be mad but I didn’t think she would hate me,” Rio said bitterly, her voice cracking. “I just- I thought maybe she’d understand, but she didn’t. She just-” She broke off, tears burning at the edges of her vision. “I’m such an idiot.”

“You’re not an idiot,” Lilia said firmly, her voice warm and steady. “You were being honest. Vulnerable. That’s brave, not stupid.”

Rio let out a shaky laugh. “Yeah, well, it didn’t fucking feel brave. Felt like getting kicked in the stomach.”

“That’s because it hurt,” Lilia said softly. “But her reaction doesn’t define the value of what you shared. You did the right thing.”

Rio swallowed hard as her fingers dug into the fabric of Lilia’s sleeve. “And Agatha, she just dumped me on some new guy without even telling me. Like I wasn’t even worth the courtesy of a conversation. Do you know how humiliating that is? After everything, she just… threw me away. I hate Agatha. And I hate me, too, because none of this would’ve happened if I wasn’t such a fucking mess.”

“And the relapse?” Lilia asked gently.

Rio stiffened again, her voice defensive. “What about it?”

“I’m not judging you,” Lilia said calmly. “I just want to understand.”

Rio hesitated, her voice dropping to a whisper. “It was too much. Everything was too much. I just wanted to stop feeling like that for a minute. I knew I shouldn’t, but I didn’t care.”

Lilia pressed a kiss to the top of Rio’s head. “It’s okay to feel overwhelmed,” she said. “It’s okay to mess up.”

“It’s not okay,” Rio said, her voice breaking. “Everyone’s disappointed in me now. They should be. I fucked up.”

“No one’s disappointed in you,” Lilia said softly. “We’re worried. We love you, Rio. That doesn’t go away because you’re struggling.”

“I just… I’m so fucking tired,” Rio said finally, her voice barely above a whisper. “Of everything. Of feeling like I can’t get it right. Of people expecting me to screw up, so I just… do it for them.”

Lilia didn’t interrupt. She just kept holding Rio, her hand moving in those steady circles on her back.

Lilia pulled back slightly, just enough to tilt Rio’s chin up so their eyes met. “You’re not a mess,” she said firmly. “You’re hurting, Rio. And that’s okay. You’re allowed to feel this way. You’re allowed to be angry and upset. But you’re not allowed to give up on yourself, do you hear me?”

Rio swallowed hard, her throat tight. “I’m trying,” she said, her voice barely audible.

“I know,” Lilia said gently, pulling her back into the hug. “And I’m here. For all of it.”

_____

Rio’s Thursday session made her seriously debate whether prison would be worth it if it meant she got to kill this guy. From the moment she sat down, Dr. Strange had been insufferable. His relentless optimism and upbeat tone were like nails on a chalkboard. Every time she shut down one of his attempts to pry into her thoughts or feelings, he’d just chuckle, like her resistance was some endearing personality quirk.

It wasn’t just his attitude that grated on her, it was the way he seemed to enjoy her frustration, like he was amused by the fact that she wanted to punch him square in his smug face. Every dismissive shrug and knowing smile chipped away at her patience. She could barely stomach the way he talked to her, like her thoughts and feelings were puzzles he could piece together if she just gave him the edges. He didn’t know her, and he sure as hell didn’t understand her.

But the moment she really lost it was when Strange said, casually and without any apparent concern for her growing animosity, “I was speaking with Dr. Harkness yesterday, actually. She passed along some notes, and we had a good discussion about your case.“

Rio blinked, her mind freezing for a second before it went into overdrive. They talked? Yesterday? About her? Her nails dug into the arms of her chair. “What the hell is that supposed to mean?” she demanded.

Strange didn’t seem fazed by her tone, which only made her angrier. “Just that she wanted to make sure I understood the progress you’ve made and the strategies she’s been using with you. She’s clearly very invested in your success.”

Invested? That word nearly made Rio laugh out loud, except there was no humor in it, just the sharp bite of bitterness. Agatha had tossed her aside and handed her off to this guy, and now she was chatting with him about her progress? Like she was a project to be handed over with a checklist of half-completed tasks?

Her mind reeled. What had Agatha told him? That she was a screw-up? That she was too much of a mess to handle? Did she tell him about their last conversation? About Nicky? About the relapse?

By the end of the session, her head was pounding, and her teeth ached from grinding them together. The moment the little clock hit 11:30 am she stormed out, slamming the door behind her.

She knew exactly where she needed to go.

Rio didn’t even realize how fast she was walking until she found herself at Agatha’s apartment building, breathless and sweating despite the cold evening air. It had been a forty- five-minute walk, but she’d made it in thirty, powered by pure, unfiltered rage. She tilted her head up, her chest heaving as she spotted a faint glow in one of the second-floor windows of her – the same window she knew was Agatha’s bedroom.

She paused. If she knocked, there was no way Agatha would let her in, not after everything that had happened. But Rio had never been one for taking the conventional route. She went inside the building, climbing the stairs two steps at a time. It didn’t take much effort to jimmy the lock open, she’d done it before, after all. She slipped inside with ease, mentally scoffing at the lack of security, even after Rio had broken in once already. Agatha really needed to invest in something better if she didn’t want people breaking in.

The interior of the apartment was dim and quiet, save for the faint creak of the floorboards under her boots. The light from Agatha’s room spilled into the hallway, and Rio stopped when she reached the doorway, her hand on the frame.

Agatha was sitting at her desk, her back to the door. Papers and folders were scattered across the surface, but it didn’t look like she was making much progress. She was staring down at something with a distracted, weary expression, her fingers idly toying with a pen.

She looked awful. The tension in her shoulders was visible, and it struck Rio in a way she wasn’t expecting.

Rio’s gaze drifted to the photo of Nicky on the desk, propped up in front of Agatha like a constant reminder. She frowned.

She cleared her throat.

Agatha jumped, spinning in her chair with a startled gasp. Before Rio could say anything, Agatha chucked the pen she’d been holding, and it bounced harmlessly off Rio’s shoulder.

“What the-” Rio stared at her indignantly. “Seriously?”

Agatha’s hand was pressed to her chest, her breath coming fast. “Rio?” she yelped. “What the hell are you doing here?”

Rio crossed her arms, stepping into the room. “What am I doing here? Did you seriously think that you could just toss me aside and expect me to simply take that lying down?”

Agatha’s lips pressed into a thin line, but before she could retort, Rio launched into her tirade.

“You’re really something, you know that? Passing me off to some random guy and not even having the guts to tell me to my face? Do you have any idea how that felt? To show up and find out you’d just… ditched me?” Her voice cracked slightly, and she hated it. “I thought you cared. I thought you actually wanted to help me. But instead, you pull this cowardly bullshit?”

Agatha’s shoulders stiffened, but she stayed silent, her gaze flickering back to the photo of Nicky on her desk. Her usually sharp features were softened with exhaustion, and the dark circles under her eyes suggested she hadn’t been sleeping. She didn’t respond, her lips still pressed into a thin line. So Rio continued;

“And what the hell was the deal with showing up at my apartment that night, huh? You were all concerned and helpful then. But the minute things got hard, you handed me off like I was too much to deal with.” Rio stepped closer, her fists clenching at her sides. “You don’t get to do that. You don’t get to act like you care one second and then cut me off the next.”

Agatha’s lips parted as if to speak, but Rio barreled on, her voice rising.

“And yeah, maybe you hate me. Maybe you think I’m a screw-up or a liar or whatever else you’ve decided in your head. And maybe I am, but you know what? I don’t care. Because Nicky deserved better than lies, and I wasn’t going to let you keep living in some fantasy where everything was fine. I told you the truth because someone needed to.”

Rio’s voice softened, but the edge of bitterness remained. “I’m sorry it’s not what you wanted to hear, but that doesn’t mean you get to take it out on me. That doesn’t mean you get to just… throw me away.”

She stopped, panting slightly, the anger still simmering under her skin. It wasn’t until the silence stretched that she realized Agatha wasn’t saying anything.

Agatha’s face was crumpling, tears spilling down her cheeks. She wasn’t even trying to hide it.

The sight of it made Rio freeze. She’d expected yelling, maybe even a fight. But this? Agatha crying? That wasn’t something she knew how to deal with.

“Look I…” Rio shifted uncomfortably, running a hand through her hair. “Don’t do that. Don’t cry. You're not meant to be sad, you're meant to be angry.” Rio paused, lost for words to say. Agatha shook her head, her hand coming up to cover her mouth as she let out a shaky breath. She wiped her cheeks hastily, as if embarrassed by her tears, but the effort was futile. “No, go on,” she said, her voice cracking but firm. “You deserve to say how you feel. Don’t stop because of me.”

Rio stared at her, frozen in the doorway. Watching Agatha struggle to keep her composure, her face wet with tears, made all the anger simmering in Rio’s chest feel misplaced.

“Uh…” Rio shifted her weight from foot to foot, her usual sharpness dulled by the awkwardness creeping in. She scratched the back of her neck. “I mean, I don’t think-”

“Rio,” Agatha interrupted, glancing up at her with glassy, red-rimmed eyes. “Will you just… sit down? Please.” She gestured to the bed behind her, her hand unsteady.

Rio hesitated, glancing at the bed, then back at Agatha. She’d spent the last half hour stomping her way here, fueled by rage and indignation. Now she was standing in Agatha’s room, faced with something she hadn’t anticipated: vulnerability.

“Fine,” she muttered, pushing off the doorframe. She crossed the room and perched stiffly on the edge of the bed, as far from Agatha’s desk as she could manage without looking ridiculous. Her hands fidgeted in her lap as she glanced around the room, trying to avoid looking directly at Agatha.

Agatha took a deep breath, steadying herself as she wiped her damp cheeks. She clasped her hands together tightly in her lap, her knuckles white. “I’m so sorry, Rio,” she began, her voice trembling but determined. “For what I said to you before, when you told me about Nicky. I lashed out, and it was cruel. You didn’t deserve that.”

Rio glanced at her, frowning, but said nothing.

Agatha continued, her voice softening as she stared at the picture of Nicky on her desk. “When you told me he’d been involved in… in all of that, my first thought wasn’t anger at you. It was that I had failed him. As his mother. It hit me like a freight train. My greatest fear, the thing I’ve spent years trying to avoid… and I still couldn’t stop it.”

Rio shifted slightly on the bed, uncomfortable with the raw honesty in Agatha’s voice.

“I didn’t want to believe it,” Agatha admitted, her voice cracking. “It was easier to think you were lying or exaggerating. But then, against my will, things started to click into place. All the little things I brushed off because I thought I knew him. The black eyes he’d say were from soccer practice. The times he said he was going to a friend’s house but wouldn’t give me a name. The nights he came home late, or the gifts he got me that I knew he couldn’t afford.” She looked up at Rio, her eyes glistening. “I should’ve noticed. I should’ve asked more questions, dug deeper. Maybe if I’d been around more, if I’d paid more attention… maybe then he wouldn’t have felt the need to look for connection in places that would hurt him.”

Rio’s frown deepened, and she leaned forward slightly, resting her elbows on her knees. “I keep thinking about my own mother,” Agatha said quietly. “How she failed me. How she chose to throw me away when I needed her the most. I’ve spent my entire life terrified of becoming her. And yet… I still failed my son.”

There was a heavy silence in the room. Rio looked at Agatha, her jaw tightening. “You didn’t fail him,” she said firmly, her voice low but steady. “You raised him well, Agatha. Nicky was a good kid.”

Agatha shook her head, tears spilling over again. “If I raised him well, then why-”

“Nicky was good,” Rio interrupted, her voice sharpening as if to cut off Agatha’s spiral. “Better than me, that’s for sure.” She sat up straighter, her arms crossing. “He cared so much about others. There was this one time that a kid, maybe he was like 12, was getting the crap beaten out of him by some older guys. I told Nicky to stay out of it, to let it go, but he wouldn’t. He walked right over there and told them to stop. And when they didn’t, they turned on him. He got the hell beaten out of him instead and I have to step in, but still. He had a big heart. He was caring and sweet, way too good for this world. And yeah, maybe he got caught up in some bad stuff, but that doesn’t mean you failed him. Every teenager looks for an outlet at that age. It just happened that the one Nicky found… got him killed.”

Agatha let out a shaky breath, her hands trembling in her lap. “You don’t understand,” she whispered. “I raised him to die, Rio. Maybe not intentionally, but-”

“No,” Rio said sharply, cutting her off. Her eyes narrowed, her voice laced with conviction. “You didn’t raise him to die. The only people responsible for his death are the ones who attacked him. Don’t put that on yourself. Don’t you dare.”

Agatha stared at her, stunned into silence.

“You gave him a good heart,” Rio continued, her voice softening slightly. “You loved him so much and he knew that. You didn’t fail him, Agatha. You gave him the tools to be better than the rest of us.”

Agatha’s sobs started anew, quieter this time, but no less raw. Rio looked down at her hands, feeling out of place “I didn’t mean to make you cry again. I was trying to, y’know, make you feel better or something.”

Agatha let out a soft, watery laugh, glancing up at Rio with red-rimmed eyes. “You did. In a strange way, you did,” she said, her voice thick but sincere. She took a shaky breath, steadying herself. “Rio, if you hate me – and I wouldn’t blame you if you did – and if you want to walk away, that’s okay. I won’t stop you.”

Rio stared at her, her jaw tightening, but said nothing. She could sense a ‘but’ coming.

“But,” Agatha continued carefully, “if you don’t… if you’re willing to stay, would you tell me more about Nicky? I-” Her voice broke slightly, but she pressed on. “I’d like to hear more about him.”

For a long moment, Rio didn’t answer. She looked down at her hands, her fingers drumming against her thigh. Part of her debated getting up, walking out, leaving Agatha sitting here just like Agatha had left her. It would be so easy.

But she wouldn’t. She could never do that.

Maybe Nicky has rubbed off on her.

She sighed heavily, her shoulders slumping in resignation. “Yeah. Okay,” she said finally. “I can do that.”

Agatha exhaled in visible relief, and she stood, hesitating before walking over to sit on the edge of the bed. She kept her distance, the space between them awkward and heavy.

Rio cleared her throat, glancing at Agatha briefly before focusing on the floor. “Uh… okay, so, Nicky.” She paused, organizing her thoughts. “He used to, uh, always beg me to listen to his favorite band. Said it’d change my life or something. God, what were they called? ‘Lorna Wu and the Coral Shore’ I think? Anyway, I gave in once, listened to a couple of tracks. He looked so smug about it, like he’d won a damn Nobel Prize or something.”

Agatha closed her eyes, a faint smile barely visible on her lips as she listened.

“And he had this weird thing about animals,” Rio continued. “One time, we were walking down some back alley, and there was this stray cat, all skinny and mangy. Nicky just stopped, sat right down in the dirt, and spent, like, an hour trying to coax it over with bits of bread. I was yelling at him the whole time to leave it alone, but he didn’t listen. Eventually, he got it to eat out of his hand. He was so proud of himself.”

Rio shifted on the bed, the awkwardness of sitting in Agatha’s space still lingering, but the more she talked the less distressed Agatha looked.

“There was this one time… he wanted to see some meteor shower. Don’t know where he even heard about it – probably some flyer at the community center or whatever. I’d seen him that morning to buy… you know… and he wouldn’t stop banging on about it. I wasn’t about to let him go alone. If you think downtown is bad in the day, you should see it at night.”

Rio glanced at Agatha, who was listening intently, she opened her red-rimmed eyes and locked them on Rio like every word mattered.

“Anyways, we climbed up to the roof of this old maintenance shed in the park,” Rio continued, her tone softening. “It wasn’t exactly sturdy, but Nicky didn’t care. We just sat there, waiting for the stupid meteors. It was cold as hell, and I spent half the time bitching about how long it was taking, but Nicky didn’t even notice. He was too busy pointing out every star in the sky and rattling off their names.”

She let out a short, dry laugh. “He even tried to teach me. Kept saying, ‘No, that one’s Polaris, not that one!’ Like I could actually tell the difference. I mean they are all just fucking stars at the end of the day right? But I was just nodding along, pretending to care. But he… he cared. A lot. He talked my ear off about constellations and meteors and black holes or some shit. I didn’t get half of it, but he was so excited.”

Agatha’s voice broke the silence, soft and trembling. “I… I taught him that,” she said quietly, almost to herself.

Rio looked up, surprised. “What?”

“The stars,” Agatha murmured, her gaze distant. “He always loved them. When he was little, he’d beg me to take him outside so we could look at the night sky. He’d make me teach him the names of all the stars and constellations. I thought he’d get bored after a week, but he never did.” She let out a shaky breath. “Even when things were hard, when money was tight, the stars were always his thing.”

Rio shifted awkwardly on the bed, running a hand through her hair as Agatha wiped at her cheeks. She was terrible at this – feelings, emotions, any of it. She was way out of her depth.

Agatha sighed quietly, fiddling with her sweater, expression turning more uncertain. “Again…” she paused, taking a deep breath to steady herself, “I’m so sorry for what I said to you. I’m sorry it caused your relapse, it wasn’t-”

“Stop,” Rio interrupted, her voice stern.

Agatha looked up, her eyes sad, apologetic, even. “What?”

“Me relapsing has nothing to do with you,” Rio explained slowly, trying to work her way around it. This topic felt too heavy, like speaking it into existence would cause it to be more real. Even if both of them knew.

Agatha stifled a sob, “But-”

“Agatha,” Rio warned, but tried to keep her voice light. “At the end of the day, it was my choice to put that needle in my arm. Not yours.”

Agatha looked at her, eyes wide like she couldn’t even believe the admission. “Still…”

Rio shook her head, “It wasn’t your fault.”

Agatha sighed softly, the tension in her shoulders relaxing slightly. Rio knew that Agatha probably felt bad, and Rio from an hour ago would probably be happy if she did. Because yes, Agatha’s words had affected her, and she wanted Agatha to know that. Wanted Agatha to hurt as much as she did.

But as she sat here, next to Agatha – more vulnerable than ever – she couldn’t find it in herself to actually put the blame on her. Seeing the way Agatha had been hurting because of the news about Nicky, she almost felt sorry for her. She didn’t want to put another weight on her.

“We shouldn’t…” She paused, not quite knowing how to phrase it. She felt out of place, being the comforter. She was so used to being the one comforted, even if she hated it. “We can talk about it another day, okay?”

Agatha let out a sob or relief and glanced at her, her expression tender but hesitant. “Can I… would it be okay if I gave you a hug?”

Rio blinked, caught off guard. “Uh… sure? If you think it’ll make you feel better.”

Agatha smiled faintly, moving towards her as she wrapped her arms around Rio gently. Rio stiffened for a moment, her body unused to the gesture, but then she let herself relax, awkwardly patting Agatha’s back.

“Thanks,” Agatha whispered, her voice soft and warm.

When she pulled back, Rio found herself face-to-face with her, their noses almost brushing. She could feel the warmth of Agatha’s breath against her lips and see every shade of blue in her tear-streaked eyes. For a moment, time seemed to stretch as Rio mapped out every feature of Agatha’s face.

Then, before she could process what was happening, Agatha leaned in, her lips pressing against Rio’s in a kiss that was soft and hesitant and full of emotion – raw and aching. Rio froze for a heartbeat, her mind spinning before her lips moved against Agatha’s in a slow, tentative response.

The kiss wasn’t rushed or urgent. It wasn’t messy or fueled by anger or desperation. It was something deeper, something that felt like an apology, a thank-you, and a question all at once. Rio let herself be held in that space, her walls cracking just enough to let it happen.

Agatha

On Saturday morning, Agatha woke up with a sore throat and stiff cheeks. She didn’t even remember crying that evening but that wasn’t a surprise - the past couple of days since her confrontation with Rio had all been a blur.

Rio telling her about Nicky had helped, whether she wanted to admit it or not. It mended some wounds, made her vision clearer – but it also made her sad. A bittersweet feeling had settled in her stomach and refused to budge.

Rio had been there for Nicky when Agatha wasn’t, and even though she was grateful for that, it hurt to know that Nicky had this whole other life, a life of which she knew nothing about.

Their kiss had felt out of place and yet so right it almost broke her heart to think about it. She hated how real it felt, how vulnerable and raw she allowed herself to become with Rio. She hated that the kiss had meant something, and Agatha couldn’t even deny it – it was clear to both of them.

After the kiss, Rio had wiped her eyes with the back of her sleeve, which had made Agatha feel a pang of guilt. She would never wish harm on Rio, she’s learnt that much over the last few weeks, and the way Rio’s eyes had looked so big, staring at her made something in her stomach clench.

Rio had left a few minutes later with an awkward goodbye. Even though Agatha wouldn’t have complained about her staying the night, it probably was for the best. After everything, she needed space. She needed a chance to think.

For two nights in a row, she didn’t have nightmares – instead, Nicky and Rio clouded every single one. She slept better than she had in years.

It was on that Saturday morning that she decided that enough was enough. She’d spent the past few days laying in bed feeling sorry for herself, and it was time for her to face the real world again. Feeling an unfamiliar determination spread across her chest, she got out of bed to wash her face.

She studied herself in the mirror, cheeks stained with tear tracks. As wrecked as she looked, she felt almost lighter, like a weight she didn’t know existed had been lifted from her shoulders. For the first time since Nicky died, she felt more than pain and anguish. The picture of him laying abandoned and helpless, dying by the side of the road that had been etched into her mind had been replaced. Instead, she was flooded with good memories: waking Nicky up for school, small talk over the breakfast table while he complained about whatever classes he had that day, the rare times he allowed himself to sink into her side as she stroked his hair while they were watching something corny on the tv.

Agatha had never thought those would be the moments she would come to miss, but those simple times were something she would give anything to have back. She would never experience that again, but oh, she was grateful that she had. To love and to lose was better than to never have loved at all.

She had just sat down to eat breakfast when an unknown number called her phone. Her stomach dropped for a second. It wasn’t unusual for people to call her, and more often than not, she did not have the contact saved in her phone.

But it was Saturday, and usually that meant it was a work call. Her friends knew her well enough not to bother her.

She really didn’t want to deal with work today. Reluctantly, she picked up.

“Hello, Agatha Harkness speaking.”

The voice on the end of the line was familiar. “I know idiot, that’s why I called you.”

Agatha let out a breath of relief, feeling her tense shoulders relax slightly. “Rio.”

“Who else would call you at 10 am on a Saturday morning?” Rio asked. Agatha could hear the smugness through the phone.

“You’d be surprised.”

Rio chuckled slightly, but her voice was tender. “How are you?”

“I’m good,” Agatha tried, the words tasted bitter on her tongue even though she spoke the truth.

“So,” Rio dragged out the word, “You’re not mad at me?”

Agatha rolled her eyes, “Not currently no. But if you don’t get to the point on why you’re calling me at 10 am on a Saturday morning, I’ll soon be.”

Rio groaned playfully, “Okay whatever I’ll get to the point.” After that came a long pause, Agatha almost had to check if Rio had ended the call. “Do you want to… you know, go on a date with me?”

Agatha’s heart stopped as she stared blankly into the wall, unsure of what to do, unsure of what to even answer. “I don’t know,” she tried, “It would be highly-”

“Unprofessional, blah blah blah,” Rio interrupted, finishing her sentence, “You can’t really say that anymore, can you? After you dropped me and everything.” Even through the light tone of her voice, Agatha could hear the bitterness behind it.

“You’re still an ex-client,” She explained, on the verge of already losing her patience. “It would be-”

“Unprofessional I know, you always said that.,” Rio interrupted again. “You kind of owe it to me, though.”

Agatha’s eyes narrowed. “I owe it to you?” She gritted through her teeth.

“After leaving me with Dr. Strange,” Rio exclaimed with a frustrated sigh, her annoyance clearly apparent by now. “Real dick move, by the way.”

Agatha rolled her eyes, but couldn’t help the smile forming in the corners of her lips out of amusement. “It’s still not… right.”

“Agatha,” Rio sighed. “Since when have you been one to care about conventional rules? I know you want to, just let go.”

Agatha closed her eyes, weighing the possibilities. She could hang up, throw her phone against the wall and scream into her pillow for the next hour at Rio’s sheer audacity. Or , she could actually just say yes, go on the date and see what happened.

Either opinion sounded awful.

“How did you get my number anyway?” She asks instead, trying to diverge from the topic.

“Alice gave it to me,” Rio shrugged. “Had to force it out of her, so please make it worth it.”

Agatha groaned, having to stop herself from slamming her forehead into the wooden table.

“Agatha,” Rio practically whined now. And oh, that made her feel something else entirely.

“Whatever,” She gritted through her teeth. “I’ll go on a date with you.”

She closed her eyes, pressing her lips tight together. Maybe this date – spending one on one time with Rio for an extended period – would be so irritating that it would quash the stupid butterflies she felt in her stomach.

On the other end of the line, Rio let out a sigh of relief. “Good, because that would’ve been very embarrassing otherwise.”

Agatha smiled despite herself, “I’m taking it back.”

“No way,” Rio complained, and Agatha didn’t need to see her to tell she had a massive grin on her face. “No take backs, we’re going on this date. I'll pick you up at 5.”

_____

Agatha stood by the window, arms crossed, trying not to feel like an idiot.

The faint hum of a car pulling up made her stomach flip in a way that was both irritating and impossible to ignore. She pressed her lips together as she heard Rio’s footsteps directly outside the door, pausing hesitantly. Agatha tried to convince herself she wasn’t nervous. She failed.

This was entirely Rio’s fault.

A few moments later, Rio knocked on the door. Agatha waited just long enough to feign indifference before opening it.

Rio leaned against the doorframe, looking far too pleased with herself. She wore a leather jacket over a dark sweater and a navy skirt that definitely was more for show than practically, her hair slightly tousled as if she hadn’t bothered to do much with it. Yet somehow, she looked effortlessly good.

Of course, she did.

“Hello,” Rio greeted, that familiar smirk tugging at her lips. "Nice sweater, by the way. I’m really into the whole 'hot librarian' thing you've got going on."

Agatha shot her a sharp look, her hand tightening on the strap of her bag. "Is this how you start all your dates? By making people regret agreeing to them?"

Rio grinned, utterly unapologetic. "Only with you. It's more fun that way."

Agatha huffed. “You’re insufferable.”

“Thank you!” Rio only grinned wider, stepping to the side to allow Agatha to grab her coat from its hanger and follow Rio out to the car.

Agatha rolled her eyes before locking the door behind her.

Rio was holding the passenger side door open for her, in a way that would have been endearing if it wasn’t for the shit eating grin that was plastered across her face. “M’lady”, she said, nodding at the open door.

Agatha scoffed. She supposed it was sweet, but she wasn’t about to let Rio know that. She got in and fastened her seatbelt, looking at Rio intently as she started the engine and pulled out from the curb.

“So, where are we going?” Agatha asked, curious. In hindsight, letting Rio take control of the entire date hadn’t been the best idea in the world, but at least she’d have someone to blame if it went south.

“It’s a surprise,” Rio said coyly.

Agatha crossed her arms. “I don’t like surprises, so how about you tell me now.”

“Not true.” Rio shot her a sly look. “Don’t pretend you didn’t enjoy the phenomenal head I gave you the first time I broke into your house. I mean, that was a surprise, and you were practically-“

“Keep your eyes on the road,” Agatha snapped, her cheeks flushing. She had wondered how long they could go without Rio bringing that night up. She checked her phone, seeing that it had been approximately two and a half minutes since Rio knocked on her door.

It was going to be a long night.

____

The car pulled into a half-full parking lot, and Rio switched off the engine, a smug look plastered across her face. Agatha glanced out the window, her brow furrowing at the sight of the building in front of them. The neon sign blinked: Community Ice Rink.

Agatha turned back to Rio, her expression flat. “No.”

Rio blinked innocently. “What do you mean, ‘no’?”

“I mean,” Agatha said, voice sharp, “there is absolutely no way I’m doing that.”

Rio tilted her head, feigning confusion. “You don’t like ice skating?”

Agatha hesitated, her lips pressing together tightly.

“Oh my god,” Rio said, her tone dripping with amusement. “You’ve never been ice skating, have you?”

Agatha straightened, scowling. “I’ve never felt the urge to strap knives to my feet and hurl myself across frozen water, no.”

Rio laughed, leaning back in her seat and shaking her head. “That’s even more reason to do it. You can’t go through life without experiencing something as iconic as this.”

“I’m fairly certain I can,” Agatha retorted. Her tone was clipped, but the corner of her mouth twitched. “Besides, this has to be against your probation. There’s no way they let you have access to a bladed tool.”

Rio rolled her eyes, unbuckling her seatbelt. “Oh, come on. What do you think I’m going to do? Use an ice skate to slice someone’s neck open? Grow up.”

Agatha raised an unimpressed brow. “Given your history? It’s not outside the realm of possibility.”

“Ha-ha,” Rio deadpanned, though her smirk quickly returned. She opened her door and stepped out of the car, leaning back in to add, “But you know, if you’re too scared to try, I totally get it. Not everyone can handle the pressure.”

Agatha’s eyes narrowed. “I’m not scared.”

Rio stood to her full height, grinning like a cat who’d caught the canary. “Hmm, sure. Whatever you say, sweetheart. I just didn’t take you for a pussy.”

Agatha’s jaw tightened, her hand gripping the strap of her bag like it was the only thing tethering her to sanity. “I am not a pussy.”

Rio shrugged, her tone nonchalant but her grin devilish. “It’s okay,” she said, her voice dripping with mock sympathy. “You don’t have to prove anything to me.”

“I don’t need to prove anything to you,” Agatha said, her tone sharp.

“Oh, of course not.”

Agatha exhaled through her nose, her pride flaring at the challenge in Rio’s eyes. She threw open her door, stepping out with an air of resignation. “Fine. I’ll do it. But if I break my neck, I’m haunting you.”

Reluctantly, she let Rio guide her inside. The cold hit her like a slap the second they stepped into the building. She shivered slightly, instantly regretting every decision that had brought her to this point.

The rink itself was mostly empty – just a few scattered skaters gliding around the ice under the fluorescent lights. She supposed that was a good thing, though it didn’t make the whole situation any less dreadful.

Agatha cast a glance at Rio, who had a spring in her step as she strode confidently toward the counter, pulling a battered pair of skates from a bag she’d apparently brought along.

“Let me get yours sorted,” Rio said over her shoulder, smirking. “Don’t go anywhere.”

“Not planning on it,” Agatha muttered, slumping onto a nearby bench. The cold from the ice seeped into the air, making the bench feel like a slab of frozen metal.

She crossed her arms and scowled as she waited, her eyes wandering idly over the rink. The sound of blades scraping the ice set her teeth on edge, and the occasional wobbly skater clinging to the sideboards only deepened her resolve that she shouldn’t have come. “Okay, here we go!” Rio sang, plopping down beside her on the bench and setting the skates at Agatha’s feet.

Agatha eyed them like they were a death trap.

Rio smirked, leaning casually on her knees. “Need help putting them on?”

Agatha’s head snapped up, her eyes narrowing. “Absolutely not.”

Rio raised her hands in mock surrender, clearly enjoying herself. “Alright, alright, suit yourself.”

Agatha gritted her teeth and shoved her feet into the skates. The laces were stiff and uncooperative, and it took longer than she’d like to tighten them properly. Meanwhile, Rio had her skates on in record time and resorted to observing her like she was some kind of circus animal.

“Take your time,” Rio teased.

“Do you want me to strangle you with these laces?” Agatha shot back, tying the final knot.

“I didn’t know you were into that stuff but I mean… if you're offering,” Rio quipped, grinning wickedly.

Agatha groaned and stood up, instantly wobbling on the unfamiliar blades. She braced herself on the bench, shooting a withering glare at Rio, who was already walking toward the rink entrance with effortless ease.

“This is going to be fun,” Rio said, holding the gate open for Agatha.

The second her blades touched the icey surface, her stomach dropped. The ground felt impossibly unsteady beneath her, and she instinctively clung to the wall, her knuckles going white.

“Oh, come on,” Rio said, laughing. She was already halfway across the rink, skating backward and making it look annoyingly easy. “It’s not that bad.”

Agatha shot her a glare that could have melted the ice beneath them. “If you’re trying to make this better, you’re failing miserably.”

Rio’s grin widened. “You look so tense. Loosen up a little.”

“I will murder you,” Agatha hissed.

“Hey, that’s my line.” Rio spun in a lazy circle, showing off. She stopped in front of Agatha, leaning casually on one foot. “You’ve gotta let go.”

“Not happening,” Agatha snapped.

Rio tilted her head, her dark eyes sparkling with amusement. “You just need to move a little. It’s like walking.”

“Walking doesn’t involve a slippery death trap under your feet,” Agatha shot back.

Rio was skating backward a few feet away, moving with the kind of grace that made Agatha’s jaw tighten in irritation. Rio had the audacity to look like she was having fun.

“Would you stop showing off?” Agatha snapped, glaring at her.

“What?” Rio grinned, executing a smooth spin and stopping effortlessly just a few feet away. “I’m just warming up. You know, loosening the joints, getting the blood flowing. You should try it.”

“I’m going to try loosening my fist in your direction if you're not careful,” Agatha grumbled, earning a laugh that echoed across the rink.

“Feisty, are we?” Rio smirked. “You’re not in control and suddenly you’re losing it. Get a grip,” Rio continued to tease, her wicked smile highlighted by the fluorescent lights. Agatha tightened her grip as if it was a life line. “And I don’t mean the side of the rink,” she added, nodding to where Agatha’s knuckles had gone impossibly whiter.

Agatha glared at her. “You’re enjoying this way too much.”

“Of course I am,” Rio said, unabashed. “You’re hilarious when you’re out of your comfort zone.”

Agatha huffed, her grip tightening. “You think you’re so funny.”

“I think I’m right,” Rio countered, leaning casually on the sideboards next to her.

Rio skated back a few feet, her hands outstretched, palms up, and her expression more sincere than Agatha had ever seen it. “Alright, take my hands.”

Agatha frowned at her, one hand still clutching the sideboard. “I don’t think that’s a good idea.”

“It is,” Rio said firmly. “Come on. I’ve got you.”

“You do realize if I fall and break something, this date is over.” Agatha warned.

Rio’s smirk softened, and she stepped closer, still holding out her hands. “Agatha,” she said, her tone quieter now, “Just trust me. I promise I won’t let you fall.”

Agatha’s eyes narrowed, weighing her options. On one hand, letting go of the wall felt like signing her own death warrant. On the other hand, Rio’s stupid, sincere tone – and her even stupider, annoyingly attractive face – were making it harder to argue.

Against her better judgment, she sighed and let go. “If you let me fall, I swear to god I’ll get you sent back to prison,” she muttered, placing her hands in Rio’s.

Rio threw her head back and laughed, a bright, unrestrained sound that echoed through the mostly empty rink. It caught Agatha off guard, stunning her into silence for a moment. She realized she hadn’t heard Rio laugh like that – a true, genuine laugh – since they’d met. She desperately wanted to hear it again.

“You’d do it, too,” Rio said, shaking her head as she adjusted her grip on Agatha’s hands.

“Damn right I would,” Agatha said sharply, trying to ignore the way her chest felt lighter.

Rio grinned, and then, without giving Agatha a chance to second-guess herself, she began skating slowly backward, pulling Agatha with her.

Agatha’s eyes went wide, and she looked around wildly, scanning for the nearest exit – or possibly the nearest hospital. “Oh, no. Nope. I don’t like this,” she said, her grip on Rio’s hands tightening.

“Hey,” Rio said gently, giving her hands a small squeeze. “Look at me.”

“I am looking at you,” Agatha snapped, though her gaze darted nervously around the rink.

“No, you’re looking everywhere but me,” Rio countered, her tone firm but patient. “Focus. Eyes on me, okay?”

Agatha hesitated, her lips pressing into a thin line, but the steadiness in Rio’s gaze drew her in. Reluctantly, she forced herself to meet Rio’s eyes. “Fine,” she muttered.

“Good.” Rio’s voice was softer now, her grip steady and reassuring. “Just keep looking at me. Nowhere else. You’re doing great.”

Agatha rolled her eyes but complied, locking her gaze on Rio’s. The chaotic noise of her nerves dulled slightly as she focused, tracing the sharp angles of Rio’s face, the curve of her jaw, the way her dark eyes caught the light and seemed to shimmer like liquid warmth.

She swallowed. Damn it, Rio was pretty. Not just pretty – she was beautiful, even now with that self-satisfied smirk tugging at her lips. It was deeply annoying.

“What?” Rio asked, her grin widening.

“Nothing,” Agatha said quickly, forcing herself to focus on the task at hand.

With Agatha no longer looking around like a cornered animal, their movements steadied. Rio’s backward skating became smoother, and Agatha could feel them gliding across the ice, the sensation oddly exhilarating despite her initial terror.

“See? Told you,” Rio said, her voice smug but warm.

Agatha desperately wanted to kiss that stupid grin off Rio’s face. But she settled on focusing her eyes onto Rio’s again, trying not to focus too much on the ice or she’d panic again. Even if she didn’t want to admit it, skating was fun. It was exciting, on the safe side of dangerous, and with Rio holding her without a single implication of letting go, it almost felt calming. “You should try without my help now,” Rio suggested, loosening her grip on Agatha.

Panic flashes through Agatha’s eyes, “No.”

“Come on,” Rio continues, “You’ll manage just fine.”

“I really won’t,” Agatha said through her teeth, looking down at the ice again. The change of posture caused her balance to almost fail, as Rio once again had to tighten the grip.

Rio chuckled slightly, but didn’t let go – Agatha almost falling was proof enough. “Fine, then I’ll teach you that next time.”

Agatha’s breath hitched, next time. To be honest, she hadn’t thought much about it, the fact that this was a date, a first date. It all felt so natural, Rio’s company completely comforting. It would've been nearly perfect if she didn’t have the constant fear of falling to her death.

“Can we get out of here?” Agatha sighed after they finished lapping around the rink, back to where their shoes are. She was already exhausted, but one quick look around the rink, she probably wasn’t supposed to be – in the time it took them to skate one lap, some people had probably finished at least ten.

“Already?” Rio said with a smug grin on her face, “Can’t wait to get me home, can you?”

Agatha rolled her eyes. Because no, she couldn’t wait, but she also couldn’t tell Rio that.

“Do you not have anything else planned?”

“Oh I do,” Rio smiled, helping Agatha to get off the ice. Agatha waited for her to continue, but she didn’t.

She felt her patience waning. “What is it?”

“You’ll just have to wait and see,” Rio waved, starting to skate backwards, “Take off your skates, I’ll just skate around for a minute.”

Agatha had no idea whatsoever why Rio would want to spend a second longer on that icy death trap. Nevertheless, she sat down on the bench with a big sigh, working to untie her laces. Her feet felt stiff and frozen like ice cubes, and her fingers could barely work around the laces.

She groaned, swearing under her breath before giving up, sitting up straight and looking at the ice. Rio was skating flawlessly, not doing tricks or anything, she was simply just skating around like she belonged there. She had the biggest smile on her lips, her eyes big and wide like a child on christmas.

Watching her made Agatha’s heart flutter. Rio seemed to belong on the ice, in a way that made Agatha think that for Rio, this was a regular hobby. Well, regular until she had been sentenced.

She wondered what else Rio had missed out on while in prison. If there were more things Rio used to love doing before, that she’d simply missed out on during those seven years. Suddenly, Agatha wanted to bring her everywhere, to see her experience all the things she had missed.

The smile Rio was carrying was contagious, and Agatha had to bite her lip to not smile too.

When Rio confidently stepped off the ice – not nearly with the same amount of struggle Agatha had minutes before – her cheeks were red and she was breathing heavily.

She had never looked more attractive.

Rio sat down on the bench beside her, heaving with her head in her hands as she tried to compose herself.

Agatha pretended like she hadn’t been staring mere seconds ago, instead leaning down to try untie the skates again. Eventually, the laces gave in and she systematically removed them.

“So, what’s the plan now?” She asked when her skates were fully removed, stretching her toes to see if they were still alive.

Rio smirked, leaning down to untie her skates with far more grace, “Dinner? Or we could just go home and cook something?”

“For a well thought out date, this one doesn’t seem that thought out,” Agatha teased. She didn’t complain though, she couldn’t. “Doesn’t really matter to me, though, you choose,” she shrugs, standing up.

Rio grinned like an idiot, “Dinner at my place, then.”

_____

Eating at Rio’s place, it turns out, equaled eating takeout – which was ironic because they could’ve just gone to a restaurant, but Agatha chose to not complain.

Rio’s apartment was cozy and inviting, which really disproved the theory that the home was a good reflection of personality. Agatha hadn’t been here since Rio’s relapse last week. Back then, she didn’t give the apartment much attention, not even bothering to look around.

But now they had finished dinner, she had time while Rio was cleaning up the table after refusing to allow Agatha to help. So, to keep herself entertained, she walked around the small living room, letting her fingers trace the fabric of the armchair and the books on the coffee table.

She raised an eyebrow at the titles: The World of Ferns, Carnivorous Plants and Their Prey, and A Beginner’s Guide to Mycology. She tilted her head. It seemed like Rio had a particular theme going on – one that didn’t exactly scream ‘dangerous ex-convict’.

“You know, I thought you would have been more into crime and horror novels,” Agatha muttered, picking up a well-worn hardcover titled Tropical Flora: A Comprehensive Study. “Your book collection makes you look like you’re auditioning to be a park ranger.” Agatha flipped open the book, scanning a detailed illustration of an orchid species.

Rio appeared in the doorway, leaning on the frame with her arms crossed and a crooked grin. “You’re just jealous I know the difference between a philodendron and a pothos.”

Agatha snapped the book shut, fixing her with a flat look. “Am I supposed to know what any of those words mean?”

Rio laughed lightly, and leaned over the coffee table, carefully rearranging the books.

Her fingers clenched around the cup, the ceramic cool beneath her touch, and she let out a shaky breath, releasing the tension in her hand. She couldn’t shake the feeling that she’d made a colossal mistake.

Letting Rio go had felt like the right thing to do at the time – like it was the only option left. She’d been so consumed by guilt, anger, and the overwhelming weight of Rio’s relapse that she’d made the decision without thinking. Without hesitation. It had felt numb, like an automatic response to the panic creeping up her throat.

She’d handed Rio off to Dr. Strange without a second thought. She hadn’t even paused to wonder how Rio might feel about it.

Dr. Strange was a brilliant mind, that was undeniable. But personally? Agatha couldn’t stand him. His ego was suffocating, he thought far too highly of himself.

Agatha pressed her lips together, the guilt hitting her with full force. Rio didn’t deserve to be handed off so callously. She hadn’t even given her a proper explanation. She hadn’t even given herself a chance to process what was happening before pushing Rio into the hands of someone who wasn’t her. Rio deserved someone who understood her, who knew the way that she worked.

Dr. Strange, on the other hand, didn’t deserve Rio. He would be too self-inflated to see the beautifully fucked up mind that sat before him. Agatha could almost picture it – their sessions, the tension thick in the air. Rio glaring at him from across the room, her lips tight, her arms crossed. And there was Dr. Strange, stroking his ridiculous beard or tapping his fingers against his desk, acting like he was giving Rio a moment to talk, all while she sat there seething.

In another moment in time she might have found the scene amusing, but instead all she felt was guilt. She hated it. She hated the thought that someone else was with Rio now. That was supposed to her role.

The idea of helping Rio only from the sidelines made her feel sick. Helpless.

She tried to rationalize it with her professional side. She cared for Rio since she was her patient, and Rio deserved the best treatment she could get. And since she’d have trouble even opening up to Agatha, she’s surely gonna have even bigger problems with Dr. Strange.

She could get Rio back. She would. She just needed to make it right. She wouldn’t tell Tony. She wouldn’t tell anyone else, not about Rio being a client again, not about their connection. Tony didn’t need to know. It would be easy to keep it a secret, just like she’d kept everything else hidden for so long.

With that thought, she pulled out her phone, her fingers hesitating above the screen for a moment. She wasn’t sure she was ready for the weight of this decision, but it was the only choice she had left. She dialed Rio’s probation officer, the line ringing twice before it was answered.

“Hi, this is Agatha Harkness,” she said, her voice steady despite the storm in her chest. “I wanted to talk to you about Rio’s therapy. I had a personal issue to sort out, but it’s resolved now. I’d like to transfer her back into my care.”

The officer paused on the other end, a brief silence filling the space between them. Then, with a tone of finality, he said, “Alright. I’ll make the arrangements. Expect the paperwork by the end of the week.”

“Thank you,” Agatha said, hanging up before the weight of her words could fully settle on her.

She stared at the phone in her hand, her resolve hardening with every passing second. This was it. There would be no more hesitation, no more second-guessing. She was going to get Rio back.

And this time, she wasn’t letting her go.
